ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e2364	Federal Minister! My question: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00123/index.shtml -- 123/M]] In the final phase of the negotiations at the Intergovernmental Conference, should it be necessary to make a decision between the right of all Member States to appoint a Commissioner in the European Commission or to maintain the current voting weighting in the Council, which option would you give priority?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e2391	Mr. Vice-Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e2407	Deputies! They now want me to choose one of two evils, and I don't want to do that. I do not want to give up on the Austrian Commissioner or a change in the weight of votes. I would also like to make a brief statement. It is for me a fundamental principle of the European Constitution, if one wants to do so – the Maastricht Treaty is, in fact, the European Constitution – that every Member State is represented in all the institutions of the European Union, regardless of whether it is the Court of Justice, the European Parliament, the Council or the Commission. And I can't imagine that the new Member States – and this issue is currently being discussed because it is now about an enlargement round – being denied the right to have a Commissioner in the very difficult initial and transitional phase. Even less can I imagine that the Greeks should, at once, refrain from a commissioner if the Cypriots have one, or that the Austrians are therefore abandoning their commissioner because a Hungarian or a Czech is sitting in the Commission. I guess that's impossible to imagine. I fear that even enlargement negotiations as a whole would be at risk if some large countries were to continue. The rebalancing of votes is also very difficult for me to understand, because the Union has worked very well so far. It was almost never the case that the big five acted or voted against the smaller ten. In most cases, a large or two large or a group of countries, the Mediterranean countries, the northern countries, those interested in the environment or others, were attached to a particular line and the others were not. And so it will be in the future. What I could imagine – at the end of the day – might be an additional certainty, namely that every decision of the Union should also have the majority of the population behind it. If a qualified majority vote takes place, then not only the votes in the Council should be counted, but it should also be checked whether about 60 percent of the European population are behind it. I can imagine that – something else not!

For example, the famous social protocol, which 14 Member States accepted – not the British – is such an example of a way out, of living flexibility. The Schengen Treaty, in which six countries participate, is a kind of flexibility. Only: In my opinion, this is a bad form of flexibility. The Treaty should rather clarify the conditions under which flexible solutions are possible in which areas. We too sometimes like to use some flexibility for ourselves. I can point to our accession negotiations, in which we have insisted that Austria must not reduce its environmental standards. This is also a flexibility. As part of this round of negotiations, I do not know if it will succeed, but I hope that we will get the majority behind us – Article 100a para. 4, which would make it possible to maintain or develop higher standards, would like to expand further. That too is flexibility. I am skeptical about the first pillar, which affects the internal market, the transport of goods and services, as I say openly. A strict monitoring system would then have to be put in place by the Commission so that we do not have a kind of "saucrumb" which is not legislatively manageable.

Scheibner: "Search"!]] [[Call to the liberals: Not enough!]] I would like to explain it once again to Mr Schweitzer. – Repetitio est mater studiorum. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In particular, there is enormous pressure from the large countries, as enlargement significantly shifts the quorum. And that's not quite incomprehensible. Name: When the EC was established with six Member States, the quota for qualified votes was 68 percent. Then it fell to 62 percent, and now it is 58 percent. And the fear of the great countries is going on: If there were to be about 11 more countries, it would fall to 50.1 percent. This could then, at some point, even allow the situation that a qualified majority of votes may be accompanied by a minority of people – therefore, the idea of the large countries to achieve a reweighting of the votes. A counter-strategy could be – as the last resort – that one should remain 58 percent at the status quo today and set up an additional barrier, i.e. any majority vote can be checked if this is required, namely whether there is a population majority of about 60 percent behind it. That's it. This is not against the Austrian interest at all, but, if you want to, it is an additional democratic legitimacy.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e3274	Mr. Vice-Chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00121/index.shtml -- 121/M]] What is the status of preparations for the EU Presidency of Austria in the second half of 1998?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e3302	Please, Mr. Vice-Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e3317	We're preparing intensively. There are interministerial working groups under the joint leadership of the Federal Chancellery and Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Organisationally, good coordination takes place, in particular by Mrs. State Secretary Benita Ferrero-Waldner. We will hold one to two European summits in Austria, probably in Vienna. There will be ten Councils of Ministers and 40 meetings at civil servants' level, which will bring something forward. In further preparation, I am already trying – that is, in advance, before we enter the Troika with January – to visit the possible crisis spots personally. We are opening three new embassies in candidate countries, in the Baltic States. I also visit all the Balkans. I was just in the Middle East and I'm going to Cyprus. I bring the report of the Constitutional Committee on Amendment 453/A by Dr Peter Kostelka, Dr Andreas Khol and comrades on a contract limitation law. ==References====External links== In a resolution in July 1996, it expressed its intention to establish, in an income pyramid, relations for all political functions in the federal, state and local authorities and self-government bodies, which are graded according to the area of responsibility and responsibility. In implementation of this resolution, the Presidential Conference took place on 5 July. In September 1996, an independent Commission of outstanding experts, chaired by the President of the Court of Auditors, was set up and entrusted with the preparation of reform proposals. This independent committee of experts submitted its report to the House at the end of 1996. In order to implement the proposals contained in this report, negotiations were started without delay, with the aim of creating a transparent and clearly comprehensible remuneration for politicians. As a result of the many hours of negotiations, which were conducted both at the expert level and at the parliamentary level, representatives Dr. Kostelka and Dr. Khol brought together on 6 June. A proposal for an initiative was submitted on 4 May 1997 on the basis of the consensus drawn up by four parliamentary clubs, namely the Social Democrats, the ÖVP, the Greens and the Liberals. Among other things, this proposal for an initiative includes the abolition of all pensionable pensions and redundancies in the area of the Federal, Länder and Municipalities, the abolition of all preemptions. Instead, there is a change in the rates according to general wage developments, as for more than 2 million workers. It also includes a classification of the remuneration according to the respective responsibilities, with maximum limits for countries and municipalities. It is the responsibility of the Land Days to lay down the terms of reference in the field of the countries and municipalities, including those of the national leaders. Furthermore, there will be a comprehensive public information on all the contributions paid from public funds, as well as the withdrawal of ceilings for several contributions and, above all, pension changes, which will result in cuts in the expectations and pensions of politicians, as no one in Austria has previously been expected. [[Abg. Böhacker: The tears come to me!]] Overall, the four-party proposal on savings in the amount of multidigit million amounts will lead to the budgets of federal, state and local authorities. [[Ironic hustle and bustle with the liberals.]] For the explanation: Only the present pension contributions, which will be lost in the medium term, amount to around 360 million Schilling. – This is so far the essential content of this draft law. We also wanted to try to find a proof of success – which is not so easy for politicians, except for elections, but not even there it always works as you know; I say this from our painful experience – to find any criteria that are objective. We have thought that the salaries could be linked, for example, to the level of gross domestic product, that they could be linked to the unemployment rate, that they could be linked to government debt, that is, to provide for changes up and down. – These were very concrete proposals to deal with them. We were particularly concerned that politicians do not have any automatic pension relationships, but that politicians are progressing with good examples and showing their own responsibility. In any case, we believe that this country should be strengthened and that it should also open up opportunities for people to show their own responsibility, i.e. to be self-responsible in pension provision. ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e10569	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I'd like to tell you a little bit about the third comparison. I spent many hours in this Commission, and it was always my special desire to ask: Whatever we decide here – what about third-party comparability? All of us who have already set salaries in our lives, in our profession, and in particular management salaries, know that it is one of the most difficult tasks to make salaries fair, fair, performance-enhancing and various other aspects accordingly. But usually there is a hierarchy in a company. There's a boss, and this boss says how much the other one should earn in the respective hierarchy level below. In this case, we, the Parliament, are the highest Hierarchy level, and we have what is even more difficult to set our own salaries. We also have another difficulty – difficult and different from the economy: There is no service component, we have no parameters on which our performance can be measured, which is suitable and can be made the basis of remuneration. We also have to say that, for good reason – because many people, many disappointed times, are suspicious and unfortunately also manipulated. We have influenced listeners. That would be appropriate. In the end, however, a later beginning is better than none, and it is an agreement borne by the spirit: not privileges, not extra sausages, but exemplary, comparable arrangements. I think that's a big breakthrough. Finally, I would like to say one more thing: Ladies and gentlemen! No doubt we will be touched by the pressure of economic development and by this cruelty, by this hardship in economic life, which Mr Wabl has also spoken about, in that the number of losers and the number of those in need and those living at the poverty line is increasing, on the other hand, a smaller layer is becoming more and more rich. We will not be able to avoid the debate on this question, and I believe it will come very, very early. It is absolutely crucial, I believe, that those who have the right, fair, just and wise laws to formulate after this debate can carry out this task without any burden on their own interests. I believe that a decisive step has been taken here that we have taken politicians harder than we can actually imagine with others – at least for the time being. That, I think, is the real advantage. We will be easier to do when we talk about civil servants' privileges, we will be easier to do when we talk about national banking privileges, and we will be easier to do if we are going to dry this gray area between politics and economy. If we succeed, then, I believe, we have also made great political and democratic progress. Thank you.

It has already been mentioned several times today: Of course, in view of the fact that there are many people in Austria who have to come out with much less money per month – unemployed, single-parents who have far too little income – there will not be a really fair solution. In conclusion: I believe that very good work has been done with this contract limitation law. It was a truly extensive task. In the national school authorities 800 management functions are with men and only about 180 with women. It will take decades if we do not come up with new approaches in this area. We need new approaches to be able to achieve the same opportunities for women. Above all, we need concrete and honest information from those responsible in order to prevent inequalities and to be able to distribute the opportunities more honestly.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e13302	The Secretary of State has spoken. Its opinion should not exceed 10 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e13323	Mr President. High house! It is true that the aim of the Federal Government is to stabilise staff expenditure. The net tax revenue, which remains to the federal government after removal to the countries and to the municipalities, is only about 400 billion schilling. Of these, about 50 percent, i.e. 200 billion schilling, are spent on personnel expenditure. That is why we have decided to stabilise these staff expenditures in the medium-term budget programme, only increasing them by about 1.3 percent each year. The figures for 1996 have already been mentioned: There was a natural departure in the federal service of about 9,000 full - time equivalents. A little less than 6,000 posts were re-occupied. As a result, a full-time equivalent of 3,500 was saved. This is the way we are going in order to achieve an increase in efficiency in a socially very responsible way and not to re-occupy every single job. For the future, we intend to continue to promote an increase in efficiency. A more detailed analysis of the 1996 figures shows that per capita staff expenditure in the public service has increased by 2.1 per cent, despite the fact that there has been no increase in the number of references. Due to the structural effect alone, i.e. biennials, there was an increase of about 1.6 percent. Further implementation of the remuneration reform cost more than half a percent, and finally the one-off payment has been added. It is remarkable, therefore, that staff expenditure per head, although there has not been a percentage increase, has increased by 2.1 per cent, while in the rest of the economy, although there have been collective contract increases there, they have only increased by 1.9 per cent. If we also want to partially compensate for this increase, we must increase efficiency in the public service. Therefore, in order to counteract this increase in per capita expenditure, jobs were reduced by around 1.7 percent in the previous year. If we still have general reference rounds from 1998 onwards, but still want to stabilise staff expenditure, we must continue in our efforts to promote the efficiency of public services. I think there is no other way to do this. However, I would also like to say clearly that we are trying not to achieve this through a work compression. I can present this briefly on the basis of the financial sector, because it has been addressed to a particular extent: Since autumn of last year, an efficiency improvement programme called "Fit 2001" has been running with us in cooperation with the staff representatives: Together with the staff representatives, building up from the individual financial offices to the Ministry of Finance, we have developed a programme of how we can work more efficiently in the future. This programme has not yet been completed, but there are already very substantial partial results. In this way, we will make very rapid progress in the computerization of all the financial offices and will definitely complete in 1998. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] As a result, it will be possible to implement the "financial online" project, i.e. that tax advisors and auditors will be able to connect directly with the financial office via computer and will thus lead to substantial savings in work at the financial office. However, we also consider further outsourcing of tasks to other professionals in Austria, for example to the notaries. Finally, I would like to mention briefly that, in line with the staff representation, we have also found that there will not be any closure of a financial office. We will only focus on a number of areas of responsibility by checking, for example, with two side-by-side financial offices whether some agendas can be more summarized in one, without affecting customer-friendliness and proximity to the public. This means that services provided by the Financial Office to citizens, to private individuals, such as the processing of family aid or other things, will continue to be provided locally. The billing for large companies, on the other hand, can easily be shifted by 10 kilometres, because a tax advisor or an auditor can be expected to do so. Of course, all these efficiency-enhancing measures, some of which also consist of non-retiring bodies, are being promoted in a non-specific way. We are, of course, in very close contact with the Women's Commissioner in the financial field. On the other hand, I did not hold in-depth discussions with Mrs Fritz and all the country representatives until about 14 days ago during a very long meeting. We have tried or tried to respond to all the concerns we have expressed. It is true that in the answer to the question, we could not give all the answers you have requested without excessive administrative burden in particular. I have tried, in order to bring some light up, at least for the central area in the Ministry of Finance, to collect such statistics myself, and I would like to briefly present to you, by gender, how retirements, the termination of service conditions by time, but also the Pragmatizations 1995/96 have been made. First, I come to retirement in the central office. In 1995, nine officials and four officials were retired from the Ministry of Finance. In 1995 nine female and a male contractors also requested the termination of their employment for the reason of the use of the pension. In 1996, 13 officials and three officials were retired and five female and one male contract staff asked for the termination of their employment due to the use of the pension. – There is therefore a statistical overweight of male servants. Second, termination of service by time: In 1995, seven contractual service contracts ended by the time period affecting all women. In 1996, two contractual relations between men and women ended by time. However, it should be noted that, despite the willingness of the service provider to extend the service, some of these terms of service were not extended by the explicit request of the staff concerned, since they had apparently already had other job vacancies. In addition, some of the temporary service contracts completed for the purpose of testing at the workplace have not been extended due to lack of service. Third point, inclusion in the public service relationship in the central office: In 1995, three men and six women were included in the public service relationship, and in the first half of 1996, five men and three women were transferred to the public service relationship. It should be noted that, in the field of central management since the summer of 1996, applications for inclusion in the public service relationship which has been achieved from that time onwards will not be made in the field of central management, since the consideration process with regard to uniform criteria for Pragmatization has not yet been completed. But this concerns men and women to the same extent. For this reason, the requests received since then by 18 female and three male staff have not yet been completed. Three of the women and two of men were employed by the retiring agencies, which were subsequently set free in the course of 1996 by retiring. In 1995 two men and one female-occupied planning posts were affected. All in all, then, I can see that all these measures have affected both men and women, and that it should not be overlooked that, especially when the term of office ends, i.e. in the case of temporary admission, at least in the two years now, but also in general, more women are affected because of the very frequent presence of representations. [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] However, I note once again that we are trying to implement these certainly painful efficiency and savings measures very evenly and unspecifically, and I can only assure you that we will always strive and strive in this direction. Thank you.

In 1988, the National Council decided on a constitutional change, according to which the Court of Auditors, in the case of undertakings and bodies subject to its control and for which there is a reporting obligation to the National Council, has to collect and report to the National Council every second year the average income, including all social and physical benefits, as well as additional benefits for pensions from members of the Executive Board and the Supervisory Board, as well as all employees, by obtaining information on these undertakings and entities. In 1988, the National Council also decided, in the context of a resolution, that, I quote, "the Federal Government is asked to work to ensure that in the field of the public economy, when new appointments are made by members of the board of directors, performance- and success-oriented remunerations are provided and that over-supply-independent remunerations are avoided." As the so-called'relation pyramid' for politicians is now ready for decision with today's presentation, it seems necessary to implement this step of a clear limitation of relations by performance and responsibilities also in close-to-state undertakings. The honourable Members therefore tabled the following motion for a resolution: [[Quoted print version removed]] Ladies and gentlemen! I would ask you to agree to this motion for a resolution, because it rounds off today's bill and also implements its basic guidelines in the areas of the economy close to the State. Only by this will the overall outcome for the public become more controllable, transparent and thus better in these areas. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-05-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00075_d7e20097	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Given the detailed discussions which have already been held in the Constitutional Committee on the matter at hand, I can keep my remarks brief. I would just like to comment on two essential aspects. One is that, with careful reading of the title of the law, one finds that a unanimous resolution of this House, according to which we wanted to remove the term "duration" from the texts of the law in the field of parental leave schemes, has apparently not been followed. In fact, there are also two laws, namely the parental leave law and the karenz leave allowance law, in which a choice of words is still used, which does not correspond to the unanimous resolution of this House. In my opinion, it would be time to recapture this line. In connection with this matter, it is not possible because it is only the titles of the laws concerned. That is why we have distanced ourselves from a corresponding amendment. The second aspect is small, but it's important to me. I also presented it to the Committee, but I do not wish to hide it in plenary either. In this novel, we create a new official title in civil service law, the official title "Military Bishop". In my opinion, this is a mistake, because only to the higher honor of the pseudo-systematics of official titles, we have a military bishop – and a Catholic military bishop is a military bishop through his ecclesial office – additionally equipped with a state title. It's, I mean, just – I don't mean to say ridiculously, because it doesn't happen – embarrassing, it's superfluous, because that doesn't change anything at all at the office he exercises in his sphere, in the sphere of the Church. He is the diocesan bishop of a personal diocese, a diocese defined by the personal characteristics of its members, the military. He is also certainly ordained according to all the regulations of the Church's law and so on. He works in military circles because it is obviously a special concern to care for people who have military service directly on the ground. So far, so good. It is also compatible with our view of separation of church and state under the title of freedom of faith and conscience that these functions are merged. But why a spiritual dignitary of a state title, which is written in an official's law, is not understandable to us. This is not a constitutional act, but has only consequences at the salary level. However, for the salary level, the fact that he is now called "military bishop" cannot be decisive, but for this his classification according to certain criteria of use is essential. This should be so, even though we believe that we are arguing that we do not treat all recognized religious communities in that country equally. That's what's added. It does not apply to comparable religious dignitaries of the recognized religious community Islam or the Israelite cult community. - That's just a footnote. I therefore ask you to support the amendment tabled by us here in the House once again, so that it can receive a majority and the corresponding passage can be deleted in the law. I would ask that, and I hope it will be possible, despite the hour ahead, to make these important repairs for us. - Thank you.

In my opinion, however, a monthly training contribution – and nothing else – is quite generous in the amount of 15 281 S, considering that other professional groups cannot build on anything comparable at all. Ladies and gentlemen! Access remains secure. There is no Numerus clausus or similar, but the reduction of the special payments. We also agreed that criteria for the training of lawyers should be laid down by the Ministry of Justice. Indeed, the qualitative and quantitative burden on legal practitioners is quite different. Administrative reform is bone work done in the "small social partners". In this context, a word on the current relationship between service providers and public service providers. In comparison, the same year, this effort for private workers increased by only 1.9 percent. That's the dynamic of the system! In order to achieve the budget objective, only 0.5 percent of posts can be re-occupied at a natural end of 3 percent of the staff. I would like to expressly say that there is an example of progress in this field in the field of education. These are new rules for calls for tenders and occupations, they are clear performance criteria, and the principal order is about co-determination of the school partnership bodies in two directions: On the one hand, the school partnership bodies issue opinions on the selection of persons. On the other hand, with regard to how they prove themselves, teachers are appointed for four years, and it is then the task of the School Community Committee and the School Authority to determine whether a person has proved himself in this function after the end of these four years. If both of them believe that this has not been the case, then this conductivity would essentially have been terminated. Some wishes are still open. In this regard, we need to have further discussions: The introduction of lists of applicants for recruitment into school so that transparency exists for all those who apply – and that is currently very many – and who cannot be taken. In this direction, we must also understand the motion for a resolution which is printed and which is also in favour of the vote. I would like to add one more amendment. The amendment has been distributed in the room; You'll find him in front of you on the pults. It is essentially a matter of including, in continuation of the discussion of the referee schemes, the national school council vice-presidents, as far as they exist in the countries – and in some cases this is the case – in this scheme. In other words, even for the vice-presidents of the National School Councils, it will be about a year at national level to appoint them at national level below the National School Council President or the President. As you know, the Presidents have 120 percent of the national councils. And when this form is completed, there will also be a reorganisation of service rights for the Vice-Presidents of the Council of State Schools. – This is essentially the content of the amendment tabled by Kostelka, Khol. Finally, the following projects: The next BDG is already in the house, it concerns university teachers and is to be decided before the summer. A larger project will certainly be the question of how Sabbathicals can be offered specifically in the field of education. The aim is also to regulate this over the next six months, so that an appropriate plan for the next school year, in autumn 1998, can be prepared. Thank you.
